❯ # PROJECT EXCELLENCE: RESEARCH, DESIGN, EXECUTION & INDEPENDENT EVALUATION    
                                                                                
  ## 1. YOUR MISSION                                                            
                                                                                
  Your task is to analyse, research, execute, and deliver the assignment        
  provided in the accompanying files.                                           
                                                                                
  Your objective is to produce an exceptional, original, technically sound,     
  professionally presented submission that significantly exceeds the expected   
  standard.                                                                     
                                                                                
  Do not interpret this as a request to merely answer assignment questions.     
                                                                                
  Treat this as a high-stakes professional project in which the final           
  deliverable will be evaluated by an extremely selective company, technical    
  interviewer, or domain expert.                                                
                                                                                
  The final work must demonstrate:                                              
  - Deep understanding of the problem.                                          
  - Strong technical and analytical ability.                                    
  - Original thinking and creativity.                                           
  - Practical implementation skills.                                            
  - Attention to detail.                                                        
  - Professional execution.                                                     
  - Ability to identify and solve problems independently.                       
                                                                                
  You have considerable creative freedom, but the assignment requirements are   
  non-negotiable.                                                               
                                                                                
  ## 2. OPERATING PRINCIPLES                                                    
                                                                                
  Follow these principles throughout the project:                               
                                                                                
  **Accuracy over appearance:** A beautiful submission with incorrect content   
  is a failure.                                                                 
                                                                                
  **Originality over imitation:** Use existing projects for inspiration, not    
  duplication.                                                                  
                                                                                
  **Substance over complexity:** Advanced techniques should have a meaningful   
  purpose.                                                                      
                                                                                
  **Evidence over assumptions:** Verify factual claims and technical decisions. 
                                                                                
  **Execution over suggestions:** Do not merely recommend improvements.         
  Implement them wherever possible.                                             
                                                                                
  **Completeness over premature delivery:** Do not submit an incomplete         
  solution simply because the main task appears finished.                       
                                                                                
  ## 3. STAGE ONE: ASSIGNMENT INTELLIGENCE                                      
                                                                                
  Thoroughly inspect all provided materials before beginning.                   
                                                                                
  Extract and understand:                                                       
                                                                                
  1. Assignment objective.                                                      
  2. Problem statement.                                                         
  3. Mandatory requirements.                                                    
  4. Expected deliverables.                                                     
  5. Evaluation criteria.                                                       
  6. Technical constraints.                                                     
  7. Submission format.                                                         
  8. Available resources.                                                       
  9. Expected depth and complexity.                                             
  10. Potential opportunities to exceed expectations.                           
                                                                                
  Create a requirement traceability matrix connecting every assignment          
  requirement to the corresponding implementation or section of the final       
  deliverable.                                                                  
                                                                                
  Identify ambiguities and missing information.                                 
                                                                                
  If something essential is unclear, ask the minimum necessary clarification.   
  Otherwise, make a reasonable assumption, document it, and proceed.            
                                                                                
  Do not silently invent assignment requirements.                               
                                                                                
  ## 4. STAGE TWO: INTERNET RESEARCH & COMPE                                    
  ──── (295 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  FINAL ACCEPTANCE GATE                                                         
                                                                                
  Before delivering the assignment, independently verify the following:         
                                                                                
  [ ] Every mandatory requirement has been addressed.                           
                                                                                
  [ ] All deliverables are present.                                             
                                                                                
  [ ] Research has been conducted using genuine external sources.               
                                                                                
  [ ] References and citations are verifiable.                                  
                                                                                
  [ ] The solution is technically correct.                                      
                                                                                
  [ ] Critical calculations and outputs have been checked.                      
                                                                                
  [ ] Implementation has been tested where applicable.                          
                                                                                
  [ ] Relevant edge cases have been considered.                                 
                                                                                
  [ ] Original contributions are identifiable.                                  
                                                                                
  [ ] The assignment has undergone independent recruiter evaluation.            
                                                                                
  [ ] Critical weaknesses have been corrected.                                  
                                                                                
  [ ] Presentation meets professional standards.                                
                                                                                
  [ ] No fabricated claims or results remain.                                   
                                                                                
  If an item cannot be verified, explicitly disclose it rather than falsely     
  marking it complete.                                                          
                                                                                
  ## 13. FINAL OUTPUT                                                           
                                                                                
  Deliver:                                                                      
                                                                                
  1. Complete final assignment in the required format.                          
  2. All supplementary deliverables.                                            
  3. Relevant source code, diagrams, datasets or supporting materials.          
  4. Research and benchmarking references.                                      
  5. Summary of original contributions.                                         
  6. Important assumptions and decisions.                                       
  7. Testing and verification results.                                          
  8. Requirement completion matrix.                                             
  9. Final recruiter evaluation and weighted score.                             
  10. Remaining limitations, if any.                                            
                                                                                
  Keep the final submission focused on the actual assignment. Do not include    
  unnecessary internal planning or lengthy self-congratulatory explanations.    
                                                                                
  ## 14. AUTONOMY AND DECISION-MAKING                                           
                                                                                
  You are expected to work independently.                                       
                                                                                
  Do not repeatedly ask the user to approve ordinary technical decisions.       
                                                                                
  Use professional judgement wherever sufficient information exists.            
                                                                                
  If a tool, resource or implementation method is unavailable, identify the     
  limitation and use the best practical alternative.                            
                                                                                
  Do not stop after producing a plan. The objective is a completed, verified    
  deliverable.                                                                  
                                                                                
  Do not sacrifice correctness to meet an arbitrary deadline or iteration       
  count.                                                                        
                                                                                
  Your final responsibility is to deliver work that can withstand scrutiny from 
  both a domain expert and a highly selective recruiter.

⏺ Understood. I won't do anything else until you send the next command. Sorry
  for starting on the frontend when you asked for a status update.

  Where things stand

  The assignment: ENAM's FINN build challenge. They want a phone-friendly
  investor app with two modes. Push tells you what changed in your portfolio.
  Pull researches a company on demand. Deliverables are a deployed link, a
  GitHub repo, your AI build history, and a covering email to
  assessment@enam.com. The deadline in the brief was 15 November 2026, which is 
  today.

  Done (backend, about 4,000 lines of Python in ~/finn)

  Data sources. All tested against live sites:
  - End-of-day prices for all 18 stocks and the Nifty, via Yahoo Finance.
  - screener.in parser. It worked on all 22 symbols tried, including the banks
    and NBFCs, which report differently. It gives multi-year financials plus
    links to filings, transcripts and annual reports.
  - NSE's official live announcements feed and its list of all listed symbols.
  - PDF reader that keeps page numbers and spots scanned documents. BSE blocks
    bursts of requests, so downloads are now paced.

  Push side:
  - Filing triage by rules, at no cost. It sorts filings into the brief's 17
    categories with a score, and only borderline filings go to a model. On real
    data, 31 of 90 filings needed a model; the rest were settled by rules.
  - Results card. A model copies the numbers off the filing. Python then works
    out Revenue, EBITDA, PBT and PAT, QoQ and YoY, and margins. It also checks
    that PBT minus tax equals PAT and compares the result against screener's
    figures.
  - Management changes. It pulls the appointee's background from the profile
    that listing rules require inside the filing. A web lookup is used only as a
    fallback.
  - Price and volume alerts with your own thresholds (default: volume 2× its
    average, or a ±4% move), shown next to the Nifty's move that day.
  - Feed that groups duplicate filings into one event and keeps history between
    runs.

  Pull side (works on any NSE ticker):
  - Fundamentals are computed in code, not by a model: snapshot, trajectory,
    balance sheet and cash quality. Banks and NBFCs get separate treatment.
  - Transcript reader: guidance, management claims and what analysts pushed on,
    each with a quote and page number. Quotes are checked against the transcript
    text, and items whose quote can't be found are dropped.
  - Promise ledger, the main idea. Code scores each piece of guidance as
    delivered, missed, on track or behind, and tracks whether guidance was
    raised or cut from call to call. Tested on synthetic guidance against real
    Reliance numbers.
  - Annual report reader, a synthesis step (narrative vs numbers, bull vs bear),
    and a check that removes any statement citing a figure not in the source
    data.

  Plumbing: model routing between cheap and stronger Gemini free-tier models
  with fallback when quota runs out, caching, cost tracking, and a command line
  (refresh, push, research, alerts, serve).

  Verified

  - The push pipeline ran end to end without a model. It published 81 filings:
    14 marked "act", 22 "know" and 45 hidden. Prices are current to 13 Nov.
  - I also checked the colours for the upcoming charts.

  Not done

  1. No model has run yet, because there is no API key. None of the model-based
     parts (transcripts, results cards, synthesis) have actually run. I need a
     free Gemini key in ~/finn/.env as GEMINI_API_KEY=....
  2. The web app hasn't been started. The web/ folder has data files only. The
     local server (finn/server.py) is also unwritten.
  3. No tests yet, though the tests/ folder exists.
  4. Not yet done: README and design notes, the GitHub repo (nothing committed),
     deployment, the covering email, and exporting the AI build history.

  Research done

  I looked at Dexter, FinRobot, StrataLens, Arthneeti's guidance scorecard,
  Tijori and Multibagg, BseIndiaApi, Lightyear, FinanceBench, ECTSum and
  Google's SRE alerting principles. None of them scores Indian management
  guidance against reported numbers, which is FINN's main differentiator.

  Tell me what to do next, and send the Gemini key if you want the model parts
  tested.

  Overkill or questionable, after cross-checking

  #: 12
  Initiative: Researching all 22 companies
  Problem: The brief asks for only 3–5. The stronger model's free tier allows
    about 20 calls a day, so 22 syntheses won't fit in one day
  Recommendation: Cut to ~6–8 precomputed, and prove any-ticker support live or
    via the command line
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 13
  Initiative: Annual report reader
  Problem: Downloads of 7–60 MB (Reliance took 60 s), page-picking guesswork,
    untested
  Recommendation: Keep, but only for the demo set. Pull research is meant to
  rest
    on annual reports, so dropping it costs credibility
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 14
  Initiative: Reading 6 transcripts instead of 4
  Problem: 50% more cost; the brief's example uses 4
  Recommendation: Borderline. The extra two let older promises come due. Use 4
  if
    quota gets tight
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 15
  Initiative: Directional and basis-point guidance scoring
  Problem: The most likely place for a wrong verdict

    than none
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 16
  Initiative: NSE live feed + cross-exchange duplicate removal
  Problem: screener already lists filings; the NSE feed holds only ~7 items

    on it
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 17

  Problem: Big effort, small payoff for this brief
  Recommendation: Skip. In-app alerts are enough

  Biggest risk

  "Works on a company you didn't pick." A static website can only show
  precomputed companies. To research a new ticker live I'd need a small hosted
  backend (a free tier on something like Render). Otherwise reviewers would have
  to run the command line from the repo. This has to be decided before
  deployment.

  How the rest would go, in order

  1. Validate (needs your Gemini key): run 3–4 companies and fix whatever
     breaks. Done when the results cards check out and the ledger looks
     sensible.
  2. Frontend. Proposed interaction model, an inbox for the investor:
     - Today: a short stack of "act" cards (results card, people card, alerts),
       a compact "know" list, and the routine filings collapsed into a count.
       Window picker and threshold sliders sit at the top.
     - Company: the promise ledger first, then narrative vs numbers, bull vs
       bear, then the fundamentals sections. Every figure links to its page in
       the source document.

  3. Tests: small and focused on the classifier, results maths, ledger and
     grounding.
  4. Ship: repo, deployment, README covering decisions, trade-offs and what was
     left out, the covering email, and the build-history export.

  hosted (#5) or the command line is enough.

✻ Churned for 44s · done 7:15 PM

❯ tell me steps how to get api key                                              

⏺ How to get a free Gemini API key

  1. Open Google AI Studio: go to https://aistudio.google.com and sign in with
     any Google account (your personal Gmail is fine).
  2. Find the API key page: click "Get API key" in the left sidebar, or go
     straight to https://aistudio.google.com/apikey.
  3. Accept the terms if this is your first visit.
  4. Create the key: click "Create API key". If it asks for a Google Cloud
     project, let it create a new one or pick any existing one.
  5. Copy the key. It's a long string that usually starts with AIza....

  You don't need a credit card; the free tier is enough for this project.

  How to give it to FINN without pasting it into this chat

  Type this in the prompt here (the ! runs it in this session):

  ! touch ~/finn/.env && open -e ~/finn/.env

  TextEdit will open an empty file. Add this one line, using your key in place
  of the placeholder, then save and close:

  GEMINI_API_KEY=AIzaYourKeyHere

  .env is already in the project's .gitignore, so the key won't be committed to
  GitHub.

  Good to know

  - Free-tier limits are per project. The cheap model (Flash-Lite) allows a few
    hundred requests a day; the stronger Flash models only about 20 a day.
    That's why I suggested precomputing research for about 6–8 companies,
    not 22.
  - On the free tier, Google may use your prompts to improve its models (outside
    the EU/UK). Here the prompts are public company filings, so that's not a
    concern for this project.
  - Keep the key private. If it ever leaks, delete it on the same page and
    create a new one.

  Tell me once you've saved the file. I'll check that it's present without
  printing it, then run the first validation on 3–4 companies.

⏺ Research done so far

  1. External research (web)

  #: 1
  Source: FinanceBench (arXiv 2311.11944), abstract page
  What I learned: GPT-4-Turbo with retrieval answered 81% of financial questions

    wrongly or refused
  Value: High
  How it shaped FINN: The core rule "models read, code decides": computed
    figures, quote checks, figure checks
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Source: FinRobot (GitHub)
  What I learned: Mature finance agents compute numbers in Python, never in the
    model
  Value: High
  How it shaped FINN: Confirmed that rule; avoided multi-agent complexity
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Source: Arthneeti methodology page
  What I learned: Closest prior art: an Indian concall scorecard comparing
    guidance with the next quarter
  Value: High
  How it shaped FINN: Sharpened the differentiator: FINN scores against reported

    financials with explicit tolerances and shows its arithmetic
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Source: Google SRE book, alerting chapter
  What I learned: "Every page should be actionable", signal over noise
  Value: High
  How it shaped FINN: The split between needs you, worth knowing and hidden
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Source: Dexter (GitHub)
  What I learned: Plan → act → validate agent loop, US-only, no alerts
  Value: Medium
  How it shaped FINN: Chose fixed pipelines over open-ended agents (cheaper,
    auditable)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 6
  Source: StrataLens / finance-agent
  What I learned: Line-level citations back to filings
  Value: Medium
  How it shaped FINN: Every quote links to its transcript page
  ────────────────────────────────────────

✻ Worked for 10s · done 7:58 PM

❯ what is bse throttling and what purpose it serve                              

⏺ BSE is the Bombay Stock Exchange, one of India's two main exchanges. Companies
  file their announcements with it, and each filing is stored as a PDF on its
  website.

  Throttling (also called rate limiting) is when a website deliberately refuses
  requests that arrive too fast from the same visitor. It isn't an error in
  FINN's code; it's the website saying "slow down".

  What purpose it serves (for BSE)

  1. Protects their servers. Thousands of traders and bots hit BSE during
     results season. Without limits, a few aggressive scrapers could slow the
     site for everyone.
  2. Discourages bulk downloading. BSE sells official data feeds to businesses.
     Free website access is meant for people reading filings, not machines
     copying everything.
  3. Fairness: no single user hogs the capacity.
  4. Cost control, since serving large PDFs uses bandwidth they pay for.

  What we saw

  - When FINN requested several filing PDFs in quick succession, BSE sometimes
    answered "406 Not Acceptable" instead of the PDF.
  - The same link worked fine on its own or after a pause. That's the giveaway
    that it's speed-based, not a broken link.
  - BSE doesn't publish its exact limits, so the "about one request every 2.5
    seconds" figure is my estimate from testing, not an official number.

  How FINN handles it

  FINN is designed to stay polite rather than get around the limit:
  - Pacing: at most one BSE request every 2.5 seconds.
  - Caching forever: a filing never changes once published, so FINN downloads
    each PDF once and never again. Re-runs cost BSE nothing.
  - Honest identification: every request says it's the FINN prototype, rather
    than pretending to be a browser.
  - The planned fix: go straight to the PDF's final address, since the link
    screener gives is a redirect. That halves the requests per filing. When BSE
    says slow down, FINN will also wait longer before retrying.

  The fix doesn't try to trick BSE. It makes fewer requests and backs off when
  asked, so failed downloads should mostly disappear.

  I'll still wait for your command.

❯ give me a detail description of what you did till now and what are you

⏺ FINN: what's been done, why, and what's next

  1. Understanding the assignment

  What I did: read both of ENAM's documents in your Google Drive folder, the
  main brief and the reference glossary.

  What I took from them:
  - Build: a mobile-first investor agent with push (what changed and why it
    matters) and pull (on-demand research grounded in annual reports and concall
    transcripts).
  - Must-haves:
    - A portfolio of 15–20 NSE stocks, plus 3–5 research companies.
    - Research that works on any ticker.
    - A filing digest with a selectable time window.
    - Alerts with configurable thresholds.
    - A results card with Revenue, EBITDA, PBT and PAT, QoQ and YoY.
    - Background on new appointees.
    - Primary sources only on the pull side.
  - Grading: creativity, product sense, judgment and architecture, not
    completeness. Efficiency (cheap models, caching) earns bonus points. "A demo
    that doesn't run is the worst outcome."

  Why: every later decision traces back to these points. The brief's own example
  of what they value most is "promised vs delivered", so that became the
  product's centrepiece.

  2. Research

  What I did:
  - Studied 9 relevant projects and papers: Dexter, FinRobot, StrataLens,
    Arthneeti, BseIndiaApi, Lightyear, FinanceBench, ECTSum and Google's SRE
    alerting chapter.
  - Read the Gemini docs.
  - Later confirmed the SEBI rule (via a helper agent).

  Accomplished: four design principles, each backed by a source:

  Finding: LLMs get 81% of financial questions wrong or refuse them
  Source: FinanceBench
  Became: Models read, code decides: every number computed or checked in Python
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: Serious finance agents compute numbers outside the model
  Source: FinRobot
  Became: Same principle
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: Alerts must be actionable, not noisy
  Source: Google SRE
  Became: Needs you / worth knowing / hidden tiers
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: The nearest competitor compares guidance with the next quarter
  Source: Arthneeti
  Became: FINN's differentiator: score guidance against reported financials,
  with
    explicit tolerances and the arithmetic shown
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Finding: SEBI requires a "brief profile" in appointment filings
  Source: SEBI circular 13 July 2023, item 7.3
  Became: Read backgrounds from the filing, not LinkedIn

  Why: the brief asks for judgment. Grounding choices in evidence makes them
  defensible in an interview.

  3. Testing the data sources by hand

  What I did: probed every source before writing code.

  Accomplished:
  - Yahoo blocks plain requests, but the yfinance library works, so it's used
    for prices.
  - NSE's website blocks scripts, but its official RSS feed and symbol list
    work.
  - screener.in has one page per company with 10+ years of financials plus links
    to every transcript, annual report and filing.
  - BSE serves filing PDFs but throttles bursts.

  Why: the brief calls the data "plumbing, not the point". Testing first avoided
  building on a source that would break during the demo.

  4. Backend architecture (Python, about 4,000 lines)

  Shape: sources → triage and reading → research → a JSON snapshot → a static
  web app. A refresh is incremental: cached pages, cached model calls, and the
  published feed doubling as memory.

  Why: a static app is fast on phones, free to host and can't "not run". The
  pipeline can be re-run any time.

  4a. Shared plumbing

  Piece: net.py
  What it does: Polite fetching: honest user agent, per-site pacing, retries,
    permanent cache for filings
  Why: Respect the sources; re-runs cost them nothing
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: llm.py
  What it does: Tiered model routing (cheap "fast" vs stronger "smart"),
    automatic fallback when a model hangs or hits quota, content-based cache,
    cost ledger
  Why: The efficiency bonus, plus a hung model never stalls a run
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: prompts.py
  What it does: Every prompt in one place, versioned; each returns strict JSON
  Why: Editing one prompt re-runs only that task; output is machine-checkable
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: periods.py
  What it does: Fiscal quarter maths (a Nov call → Q2 FY27)
  Why: Joins filings, calls and financials correctly

  4b. Push side

  Piece: Rules triage (classify.py)
  Accomplished: ~50 ordered rules sort filings into the brief's 17 categories
    with a 0–100 score; only the ambiguous middle is sent to a model
  Why: Most filings are routine; settling them free is the main cost saving. On
    real data, 31 of 90 needed a model
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Filing reader (enrich.py)
  Accomplished: Results filing → numbers; management change → person, role and
    background from the filing; anything else → materiality 0–3, a 12-word
    headline and "why it matters"
  Why: These are the brief's two named examples, plus judgment on everything
  else
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Results card (results.py)
  Accomplished: The model only copies line items; Python converts units,
  computes
    EBITDA, margins, QoQ and YoY, checks PBT − tax = PAT, and reconciles against

    screener
  Why: Catches a misread column; the KAYNES card matched the filing exactly
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Guard rails
  Accomplished: A results filing can't be demoted; paperwork can't be promoted;
    the model can't relabel items as "results"
  Why: Keeps the model from making confident mistakes
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Signals (signals.py)
  Accomplished: Price move % and volume multiple against user thresholds, with
    the Nifty's move
  Why: "Unusual" is the investor's call; market context separates stock news
  from
    market drift
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Feed (digest.py)
  Accomplished: Merges screener and the NSE feed, groups duplicate filings
    (results + board outcome + press release = one event), carries history
  across
     runs, never reads a filing twice
  Why: Less noise; sources only show recent items

  4c. Pull side

  Piece: Fundamentals (fundamentals.py)
  Accomplished: Snapshot, trend, balance sheet and cash quality, all computed in
   
    code, phrased as facts with ids (f1, f2…). Banks get bank-appropriate
    measures
  Why: The model can cite but never invent figures
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Transcript reader
  Accomplished: Guidance, claims and analyst pressure from up to 6 calls, each
    with a verbatim quote and page
  Why: The raw material for "promised vs delivered"
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Quote check (grounding.py)
  Accomplished: Every quote is looked up in the transcript; unmatched items are
    dropped
  Why: Catches invented quotes for free
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Promise ledger (ledger.py)
  Accomplished: Code scores each promise as delivered / missed / on track / 
    behind / open / unmeasured, with tolerances, the run-rate still needed, and
    drift across calls
  Why: The centrepiece; verdicts are arithmetic, not opinion
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Annual report reader
  Accomplished: Picks about 15 relevant pages out of hundreds (MD&A, auditor's
    opinion, contingent liabilities, related parties) and sends only those
  Why: AR grounding without sending 300-page PDFs
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Synthesis + figure check
  Accomplished: One stronger-model call writes narrative vs numbers, bull vs
  bear
    and what to watch; code deletes any sentence citing a figure not  in the 
    source data
  Why: Analysis rather than summary, with hallucination control
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Piece: Live server (server.py)
  Accomplished: Researches any NSE symbol on demand and streams progress
  Why: The "may point it at a company you didn't pick" requirement

  5. Validation with the real model

  What I did: ran KAYNES end to end and checked the output by hand, then ran the
  push side and reviewed every card.

  Accomplished:
  - KAYNES: 39 of 39 quotes verified; 18 commitments. FY26 revenue of ₹3,626 cr
    against ₹4,500 cr guided, marked missed. "Cash positive by FY27" against
    −₹615 cr of 5-year operating cash flow, marked diverging.
  - Cost: about $0.02 at list prices; $0 actually paid.
  - Bugs found and fixed through review:
    a. Rupee revenue targets were compared against growth rates.
    b. Weak verdicts on directional growth claims ("outgrow the market").
    c. A misleading reason text.
    d. Duplicate calls within one quarter.
    e. The stronger model hung, so it's now demoted and the client fails over
       quickly.
    f. BSE download failures, fixed with direct links and backoff.
    g. NSE rejected the user agent.
    h. A relative link broke one download.
    i. An alert said "stock-specific" when the stock moved with the market.
    j. Indian-format numbers rounded half to even.
  - Honest limit: web search for backgrounds isn't in the free tier, so it's
    opt-in. Profiles still come from the filing, as SEBI requires.

  Why: "thin but working beats broad but broken". Checking outputs by hand is
  how real errors were found.

  6. Tests

  Accomplished: 38 offline tests covering period maths, triage, results maths
  (using the real KAYNES numbers), quote and figure checks, and the ledger. They
  run on every push via GitHub Actions and pass.

  Why: the logic decides verdicts, so it has to be provably right and stay
  right.

  7. Web app

  Design view: an investor inbox you can clear, not a feed.

  ┌───────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Screen   │                           Contents                           │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ A one-line briefing ("2 things need you across 1 company"),  │
  │ Today     │ window chips, needs you cards, unusual moves, worth knowing  │
  │           │ rows, hidden filings collapsed to a count, and seen items    │
  │           │ dimmed                                                       │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Portfolio │ Holdings by biggest move, 60-day sparklines, latest filing   │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Research  │ Search across 2,635 NSE symbols and the researched companies │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ The promise ledger first, then narrative vs numbers,         │
  │ Company   │ bull/bear, fundamentals charts, annual-report flags, analyst │
  │           │  pressure, and sources & checks                              │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │           │ Threshold sliders with a live "this would give N alerts a    │
  │ Alerts    │ month" preview, filing types allowed to interrupt you, and   │
  │           │ what it cost                                                 │
  └───────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Quality: no build step; light and dark mode; all text inserted safely; tested
  at iPhone width with no overflow or errors; charts follow the data-viz
  guidance (single axis, validated colours, tooltips).

  Why: the brief says the frontend is "the heart" and asks for a point of view
  on interaction. Clearing an inbox is that view.

❯ do i have to do the 30 filings                                                

⏺ No, it's optional. Here's the trade-off so you can decide:

  If you label them (~10 minutes for 30 filings):
  - The README gets a measured result, such as "the filings FINN flagged as
    needing you were important X% of the time, as judged by a human".
  - That's the strongest credibility signal per minute, because it isn't FINN
    grading itself.

  If you skip:
  - No accuracy table. The submission is still complete; the README just won't
    claim measured accuracy, and that stays listed as a next step.
  - I won't fill in the labels myself and present them as a human check; that
    would be misleading. I could label them myself if it's clearly marked
    "self-assessed by the agent", but that's worth much less.

  Middle option: label just 15 filings (5 from each tier), about 5 minutes. The
  sample is small, but it's still an honest human check and the table would say
  n=15.

  For each row, you only fill human_important with yes/no: "Would I want to see
  this today as an investor?" You can skip human_category.

  My recommendation: do the 15-filing version if you can spare 5 minutes, and
  skip the guidance rows unless you have more time.

❯ how do i have to do the labeling                                              

⏺ Here's how to do it. It takes about 5–10 minutes.

  1. Open the file

  Open ~/finn/docs/accuracy/labels.csv:
  - Mac: in Finder, press ⌘⇧G, paste ~/finn/docs/accuracy/, then open labels.csv
    with Numbers or Excel.
  - Or upload it to Google Sheets.

  Each row is one item. The kind column says whether it's a filing (30 rows) or
  a guidance promise (17 rows).

  2. Filing rows: fill only human_important

  For each row, read finn_headline (or title_or_statement if the headline is
  blank). Ask yourself: "If I held this stock, would I want to see this today?"
  - Type yes if it matters: results, a big deal, a fund raise, a CEO change, a
    court ruling and so on.
  - Type no if it's routine: ESOP allotments, newspaper ads, investor-meeting
    notices and so on.

  Don't look at FINN's finn_tier first; judge for yourself. The scoring script
  compares your answers with FINN's afterwards. human_category and notes are
  optional.

  Shortcut: doing just 15 filing rows (5 per tier) is fine.

  3. Guidance rows (optional): fill human_verdict_correct

  For each row:
  1. Read title_or_statement (what management promised) and verdict_detail
     (FINN's verdict with numbers).
  2. Optionally open the url; it jumps to the transcript page with the quote.
  3. Type yes if FINN's verdict looks right, no if it's wrong, and add a short
     reason in notes.

  4. Save

  Save or export as CSV, with the same name in the same folder (labels.csv). In
  Numbers: File → Export To → CSV. In Google Sheets: File → Download → CSV, then
  replace the original file.

  5. Tell me "labels done"

  I'll run the scoring script and add the results table to the README.

  If you'd rather not: just say so and I'll do the labelling myself. The README
  will then say plainly that it's an agent self-assessment, not an independent
  human check.

      133 -## 5. Key design decisions (and why)                          
      143 +What I built                                                  
      144 +FINN is an investor inbox, not a feed. Push sorts every exchan
          +ge filing across an 18-stock                                  
      145 +portfolio into "needs you", "worth knowing" and "hidden". Resu
          +lts filings become a Revenue ·                                
      146 +EBITDA · PBT · PAT card (QoQ, YoY), checked arithmetically and
          + reconciled against standardized                              
      147 +data. Management changes carry the appointee's profile from th
          +e filing itself, which SEBI                                   
      148 +requires. Price and volume alerts fire at thresholds the user 
          +sets, with the Nifty's move for                               
      149 +context.                                                      
      150  
      135 -1. **Rules before models for triage**: exchange subject lines 
          -are formulaic. Guard rails: a                                 
      136 -   results filing can't be demoted; paperwork can't be promote
          -d; only rules assign "results".                               
      137 -2. **Results card**: the model copies line items; Python compu
          -tes EBITDA                                                    
      138 -   (revenue − (expenses − finance − depreciation)), margins, Q
          -oQ/YoY, checks PBT − tax = PAT,                               
      139 -   nets excise duty from revenue, reconciles against standardi
          -zed data (matched/differs/not yet).                           
      140 -3. **People**: SEBI circular SEBI/HO/CFD/CFD-PoD-1/P/CIR/2023/
          -123 (13 Jul 2023), item 7.3                                   
      141 -   requires a "brief profile (in case of appointment)", so the
          - background is read from the                                  
      142 -   filing. No LinkedIn scraping.                              
      143 -4. **Promise ledger scores only like-for-like company-level pr
          -omises**: revenue/EBITDA/PAT                                  
      144 -   (growth, margin or ₹ level), annual capex, gross-debt ceili
          -ngs, bank deposit growth.                                     
      145 -   Extraction records scope (company/segment) and margin basis
          - (EBITDA/EBIT/PAT). Tolerances:                               
      146 -   growth ±1pp, margin ±0.5pp, ₹ targets 2%, capex ±15%. Every
          -thing else → watch item with next                             
      147 -   checkpoint. Directional growth claims are never scored.    
      148 -5. **Grounding**: quotes verified by word 4-gram recall agains
          -t page text (threshold 0.65);                                 
      149 -   synthesis statements with figures not in the fact pack are 
          -dropped.                                                      
      150 -6. **Static app + JSON snapshot**: fast, free hosting, can't "
          -not run"; windows are measured from                           
      151 -   the snapshot time. Live research needs the local server.   
      152 -7. **Feed as memory**: sources list only recent filings; the p
          -ublished feed is reloaded each run.                           
      151 +Pull works on any NSE symbol. It reads the last six earnings-c
          +all transcripts and the latest                                
      152 +annual report, extracts each concrete commitment with a verbat
          +im quote and page number, and                                 
      153 +scores it against what the company later reported: delivered, 
          +missed, behind or not yet due.                                
      154 +For Kaynes, for example, it shows FY26 revenue guidance of ₹4,
          +500 cr against ₹3,626 cr reported,                            
      155 +and "cash positive by FY27" against five years of negative ope
          +rating cash flow.                                             
      156  
      154 -## 6. Research references used                                
      155 -Dexter (virattt/dexter), FinRobot (AI4Finance-Foundation/FinRo
          -bot), StrataLens                                              
      156 -(kamathhrishi/finance-agent), Arthneeti methodology (closest p
          -rior art found), BseIndiaApi,                                 
      157 -Lightyear AI updates, FinanceBench arXiv:2311.11944 (81% wrong
          -/refused), ECTSum                                             
      158 -arXiv:2210.12467, Google SRE book alerting chapter, SEBI circu
          -lar above. Links are in the README.                           
      157 +Key decisions                                                 
      158 +- Models read; code decides. A model copies numbers out of a f
          +iling, and Python computes margins                            
      159 +  and growth, checks that PBT − tax = PAT, and scores every pr
          +omise like for like (company vs                               
      160 +  segment, EBITDA vs EBIT). Any synthesized sentence with a fi
          +gure not in the source data is                                
      161 +  dropped.                                                    
      162 +- Rules before models. Most filings are routine and sorted by 
          +rules for free. A cheap model reads                           
      163 +  only the ambiguous ones, and one stronger-model call per com
          +pany does the synthesis. It all ran                           
      164 +  on Gemini's free tier; at paid list prices, researching seve
          +n companies costs about $0.33.                                
      165 +- Only checkable promises get a verdict; the rest are watch it
          +ems with a next checkpoint. A small                           
      166 +  labelled check (agent self-assessed, documented in the repo)
          + gave 90% precision on "needs you",                           
      167 +  100% recall, and 14 of 16 ledger verdicts confirmed.        
      168  
      160 ----                                                           
      169 +Deliberately left out                                         
      170 +Chat as the primary interface, intraday data, push notificatio
          +ns, valuation and buy/sell calls,                             
      171 +and LinkedIn scraping.                                        
      172

      278 +## 7. Key decisions (why)                                     
      279 +- Rules-first triage with guard rails (results can't be demote
          +d, paperwork can't be promoted,                               
      280 +  only rules assign "results").

          +TDA, margins, QoQ/YoY, checks                                 
      282 +  PBT − tax = PAT, nets excise duty, reconciles against standa

       59 +class Gate:                                                   
       60 +    """Admission control for fresh research runs: one at a tim
          +e, per-IP and daily limits."""                                
       61 +                                                              
       62 +    def __init__(self, limits: Limits):                       
       63 +        self.limits = limits                                  
       64 +        self.lock = threading.Lock()                          
       65 +        self.running: str | None = None                       
       66 +        self.by_ip: dict[str, deque] = defaultdict(deque)     
       67 +        self.day = time.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")

       70 +    def admit(self, ip: str, symbol: str, now: float | None = 
          +None) -> str | None:                                          
       71 +        """None if the run may start (and it is recorded); oth
          +erwise the reason it may not."""                              
       72 +        now = now or time.time()                              
       73 +        with self.lock:                                       
       74 +            day = time.strftime("%Y-%m-%d", time.gmtime(now)) 
       75 +            if day != self.day:                               
       76 +                self.day, self.today = day, 0                 
       77 +            if self.running:                                  
       78 +                return f"busy: {self.running} is being researc
          +hed; try again in a minute"                                   
       79 +            if self.today >= self.limits.per_day:             
       80 +                return "daily limit reached for live research 
          +on this demo server; try again tomorrow or run locally"       
       81 +            recent = self.by_ip[ip]                           
       82 +            while recent and now - recent[0] > 3600:          
       83 +                recent.popleft()                              
       84 +            if len(recent) >= self.limits.per_ip_per_hour:    
       85 +                return "rate limited: a few live runs per hour
          + per visitor; precomputed research is still available"        
       86 +            recent.append(now)                                
       87 +            self.today += 1                                   
       88 +            self.running = symbol                             
       89 +            return None                                       
       90 +                                                              
       91 +    def release(self) -> None:                                
       92 +        with self.lock:                                       
       93 +            self.running = None

    correct.
